def _optimize_coordinate(h, c, n, t0, budget, rng, topK=16, max_pairs=20000):
    h = h.copy(); c = _full_c(h)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cmax
    L=2*n-1
    h_rev = h[::-1]
    while time < budget:
       # recompute cmax maybe c normalized
       if cmax < best_c: update best
       # gradient active
       g = _grad_active(h,c,topK)
       # candidate set
       candidates = []
       P = min(12, n//2)
       pos = np.argsort(g)[-P:]
       neg = np.argsort(g)[:P]
       # add pos-neg pairs
       for a in pos:
          for b in neg:
             if a!=b: candidates.append((a,b))
       # random pairs
       for _ in range(n_random): a=rng.randint(n); b=rng.randint(n); if a!=b: append
       # maybe pair i with (i+shift) random shifts
       # Deduplicate? Use set maybe overhead. For small list, okay.
       accepted=0

              # update h_rev? h changed; recompute h_rev? Could update h slice? For next candidate, A formula uses current h. Change only two entries.        if accepted==0 and not first round:

Line search function:
```
def _line_pair(h,c,i,j,t1,t2, tmp, iters=30):
    n=len(h); L=len(c); low=max(-h[i], h[j]-1.0); high=min(1.0-h[i], h[j])
    if low > high - 1e-14: return 0, float('inf')
    low += 1e-15; high -=1e-15? For bounds.
    # if current cmax is 0? no.
    # Quick evaluate at current 0? cmax known.
    one_minus_rev = 1.0 - h[::-1]
    A = np.zeros(L)
    A[i:i+n] += one_minus_rev
    A[n-1-i:n-1+i+1] -= h
    A[j:j+n] -= one_minus_rev
    A[n-1-j:n-1+j+1] += h
    D = abs(i-j)
    t1 = n-1+D; t2 = n-1-D  # both within 0..L-1
    # define f(delta): tmp = c + A*delta; max with tmp[t]+delta^2
    gr,gl? Golden:
    invphi = (3-np.sqrt(5))*0.5
    c1=high- (high-low)*invphi; c2=low+(high-low)*invphi? Standard.
    f1=f(c1); f2=f(c2)
    for _ in range(iters):
       if f1 < f2:
          high=c2; c2=c1; f2=f1; c1= high - (high-low)*invphi; f1=f(c1)
       else:
          low=c1; c1=c2; f1=f2; c2= low + (high-low)*invphi; f2=f(c2)
    delta=0.5*(low+high); fbest=min(f1,f2,f(delta))
    return delta,fbest
```

`_grad_active` implementation:
```
def _grad_active(h,c,k):
    n=len(h); L=len(c)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, L-k)[-k:] if k<L else np.arange(L)  # maybe sort
    # weights maybe inverse distance to max? Could set w=1 or exp alpha.
    # If want weights: cmax=c.max(); w=np.exp((c[idx]-cmax)/scale); normalize. Simpler uniform.
    g=np.zeros(n)
    for t in idx:
       d=t-(n-1)
       if d>=0:
          m=n-d
          if m<=0: continue
          g[d:n] += 1.0 - h[:m]
          g[:m] -= h[d:n]
       else:
          e=-d; m=n-e
          g[:m] += 1.0 - h[e:n]
          g[e:n] -= h[:m]
    return g
```
Uniform top K. For weighted, need scalar per t. Could do:
```
order = np.argsort(c)
top = order[-K:]
w = (c[top] - c[top[-1-1]?])? But c sorted ascending top[-1] max. Use w = np.exp(alpha*(c[top]-cmax)) perhaps.
```
Uniform top K adequate.

But gradient of max maybe should include all lags within tolerance, not just top K. Could choose K = max(8, int(0.02*L)) maybe 20. Or adaptive: K all lags where c > cmax - tau, tau = max(1e-6, 0.001*(cmax))? For n=560, maybe many. But building loop over many lags expensive. Use K=20. We can also use logsum weights for all? Too heavy. Could compute weighted gradient over all lags using FFT? Not necessary.

Can compute subgradient of exact max by taking active lags with c = cmax (within 1e-12). Usually few. But top K enough.

def _coord_opt(h, t0, stop, rng, topK=16, grad_pairs=8, random_pairs=40, max_iters=1000):
    n=len(h); h=h.copy(); c=_full_c(h); cmax=float(c.max()); L=2*n-1
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cmax
    workA=np.empty(L); workV=np.empty(L)
    accepted_total=0
    no_improve=0
    while time.time() < stop:
        g=_grad_active(h,c,min(topK,L))
        # candidate pairs set
        pairs=[]
        P=min(grad_pairs, n)
        if P>1:
           pos=np.argsort(g)[-P:]
           neg=np.argsort(g)[:P]
           # maybe also top absolute? Use pos/neg cross.
           for a in pos:
              for b in neg:
                 if a!=b: pairs.append((a,b))
        # random
        R=random_pairs
        for _ in range(R):
           a=rng.randint(n); b=rng.randint(n); if a!=b: pairs.append((a,b))
        # maybe shift pairs: for k in shifts: a=rng, b=(a+shift)%n? Could add.
        # local ambiguity: pair extreme high h? maybe.
        impr=0
        # limit per sweep to avoid stale gradient too much: max_cand_per_sweep = len(pairs) but maybe.
        for (i,j) in pairs:
            if time.time() > stop: break
            delta, val = _line_search_pair(h,c,i,j,workA,workV)
            if val < cmax - 1e-11:
                # update
                hi = h[i] + delta; hj = h[j] - delta
                if hi < 0: hi=0; delta=-h[i]; if ...
                if hi > 1: hi=1; delta=1-h[i]
                if hj < 0: hj=0; delta=h[j]
                if hj > 1: hj=1; delta=h[j]-1
                # If clipped, maybe recompute delta? We can trust line search bounds; but round.
                h[i]=hi; h[j]=hj
                # update c: c += A*delta; but if clipped delta changed from val? If no clip, fine. If clipped, maybe don't update? We can check if abs(delta - (hi-h[i_old])) >1e-12; then recompute line? Simpler ensure line search returns within [lo,hi] with small shrink; no clip.
                q=delta*delta
                workV += c? Wait update uses A: workA currently A? But _line_search_pair may leave workA as A? It modifies workA to A, then uses workV; workA remains A. We can use workA for update. But if helper returns and workA still A. Good.
                c += workA * delta  # This allocates temp? `workA * delta` creates. Better np.add(c, workA*delta, out=c) still temp. Could do `c += workA * delta`; allocation length L. Accept. Or use np.multiply(workA, delta, out=workV); np.add(c, workV, out=c)
                c[t1] += q; c[t2]+q (need t1/t2; _line_search_pair doesn't return t1/t2? It can return tD? Or compute here D=abs(i-j); t1=n-1+D; t2=n-1-D)
                cmax = float(c.max())
                impr +=1
                if cmax < best_c: best_c=cmax; best_h=h.copy()
                # occasionally recompute c? maybe after 100 updates within sweep
                if impr % 200 ==0: c=_full_c(h); cmax=c.max()
        # after sweep, if no improvement, maybe perturb
        if impr==0:
            # random perturbation with small step? Or change topK.
            # Do a few random bound moves: choose random pair and apply step towards line min even if worse? For escape.
            for _ in range(20):
                i=rng.randint(n); j=rng.randint(n); if i==j continue
                # find full interval midpoint random? maybe choose delta = 0.1 * random sign clipped
                delta = 0.05*rng.randn()
                lo,hi = bounds; delta = min(max(delta,lo),hi)
                h[i]+=delta; h[j]-=delta
                c=_full_c(h); cmax=c.max()
                # accept if not too worse? but line search min? For escape, maybe accept with probability exp(-Δ/temperature). We'll keep if lower else revert? Random perturbation can find new basin but may increase.
            # To avoid losing, keep best.
```
But random perturbation ruins best? We can operate on working h and only revert to best if no improve after many? Need not. We can include simulated annealing? Maybe overkill.

def _perturb(h, amp, rng):
   h = h.copy(); mask = rng.rand(n) < 0.2; h[mask] += amp*rng.randn(mask.sum()); return _proj_box_sum(h,n/2)
```

```
def _coord_opt(h, t0, stop, rng, line_iters=22):
    n = len(h)
    h = _repair(h,n).astype(float, copy=True)
    c = _full_c(h)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cmax
    L = 2*n-1
    workA = np.zeros(L)
    workV = np.zeros(L)
    target = n*0.5
    # adaptive params
    if n < 80: topK=8; P=3; Rpairs=40; pamp=0.15
    elif n < 200: topK=12; P=5; Rpairs=70; pamp=0.10
    else: topK=18; P=7; Rpairs=110; pamp=0.07
    no_impr=0; restart=0; max_restarts=20
    acc_sweep=0
    while time.time() < stop:
        # maybe recompute cmax if inconsistency
        g = _grad_active(h,c,topK)
        # candidate pairs: pos/neg + random
        # Instead of building list (memory), process on fly.
        # We can process pos/neg:
        pos = np.argsort(g)[-P:]
        neg = np.argsort(g)[:P]
        impr=0
        for a in pos:
           for b in neg:
              if a==b: continue
              if _try_pair(h,c,workA,workV,...): impr++; update
        for _ in range(Rpairs): ...
        # Maybe if impr == 0, try more random 3x
        if impr == 0:
            for extra random...
        if impr > 0:
            no_impr = 0
        else:
            no_impr += 1
            if no_impr >= 2:
                # perturb best_h
                h = _perturb_around(best_h, pamp, rng, target)
                c = _full_c(h); cmax=float(c.max())
                if cmax < best_c: best_c=cmax; best_h=h.copy()
                no_impr=0; restart+=1
                if restart > max_restarts: break? maybe perturb amplitude larger gradually.
        # occasionally rebuild c due drift
        if acc_sweep % 100 ==0... But acc_sweep global; after 200 accepted, recompute.
    h=best_h? Return best_h and c5 integral? Return h copy and cmax sum. But if best sum maybe need recompute. Return best_h, _eval(best_h)
```

```
def _line_search_pair(h, c, i, j, workA, workV, iters):
    n=len(h); L=len(c)
    hi_old=h[i]; hj_old=h[j]
    lo = max(-hi_old, hj_old - 1.0)
    hi = min(1.0 - hi_old, hj_old)
    if hi - lo < 1e-14: return 0.0, float(c.max()), 0,0
    # shrink interval to keep inside
    lo += 1e-12*(hi-lo) maybe? Use 1e-12.
    hi -= 1e-12*(hi-lo)
    h_rev = h[::-1]
    omr = 1.0 - h_rev
    workA.fill(0.0)
    workA[i:i+n] += omr
    workA[n-1-i:n-1+i+1] -= h
    workA[j:j+n] -= omr
    workA[n-1-j:n-1+j+1] += h
    D = abs(i-j)
    tpos = n-1 + D
    tneg = n-1 - D
    # Golden
    gr=0.5*(3.0-np.sqrt(5.0))
    c1 = hi - gr*(hi-lo)
    c2 = lo + gr*(hi-lo)
    f1 = _line_value(workV,c,workA,tpos,tneg,c1)
    f2 = _line_value(workV,c,workA,tpos,tneg,c2)
    for _ in range(iters):
       if f1 < f2:
          hi = c2; c2 = c1; f2=f1; c1 = hi - gr*(hi-lo); f1=_line_value(...)
       else:
          lo = c1; c1 = c2; f1=f2; c2 = lo + gr*(hi-lo); f2=_line_value(...)
    delta = 0.5*(lo+hi)
    f3 = _line_value(...)
    if f1 < f3: delta=c1; fbest=f1 ...
    return delta, fbest, tpos, tneg
```
But `omr = 1.0 - h[::-1]` creates array; h[::-1] view; okay. Need ensure workA slices when i+n? For i=n-1, i+n=2n-1, length n, OK. n-1+i+1 = 2n-1? n-1+n-1+1 = 2n-1, exclusive index 2n-1? L=2n-1 last index 2n-2; slice end 2n-1 includes last. Good.
For j second slice: n-1-j:n-1+j+1, for j=n-1 end = n, start=0, length n, OK; j=0 start=n-1,end=2n-1, length n.

`_line_value`:
```
def _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, delta):
    np.multiply(A, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    m = float(work.max())
    q = delta*delta
    if q != 0.0:
       v1 = work[t1] + q
       if v1 > m: m = v1
       if t2 != t1:
          v2=work[t2]+q
          if v2>m: m=v2
    return m
```
It doesn't restore work but next multiply overwrites (out=work overwrites all entries). Good.
Potential issue: t1,tneg maybe out of bounds? D between 1 and n-1; n-1-D >=0, n-1+D <=2n-2. Good.

Let's define:
```
def _make_A(h,i,j,workA):
    workA.fill(0.0); n=len(h); omr=1.0-h[::-1]
    workA[i:i+n] += omr
    workA[n-1-i:n-1+i+1] -= h
    workA[j:j+n] -= omr
    workA[n-1-j:n-1+j+1] += h
    return abs(i-j)
```
Note `omr` is array; slice += omr modifies. Since `omr` new each call.

`_line_search_with_A(h, c, workA, tpos, tneg, workV, lo, hi, iters)` uses c/A. It doesn't need h except for bounds? Bounds precomputed.

def _coord_opt(h, t0, stop, rng, line_iters=None, max_restarts=30):
    n=len(h); target=0.5*n; h=_repair(h,n).copy(); c=_full_c(h); cmax=float(c.max()); L=2*n-1
    best_h=h.copy(); best_c=cmax
    workA=np.zeros(L); workV=np.zeros(L)
    if line_iters is None:
       line_iters = 20 if n<200 else 24
    P = 4 if n<100 else 7
    Rpairs = 30 if n<100 else 90
    topK = 8 if n<100 else 16
    act_tol = 1e-9
    active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
    no_impr=0; restart=0; acc_sweep=0; acc_total=0
    while time.time() < stop:
       g=_grad_active(h,c,min(topK,L))
       pos=np.argsort(g)[-P:]
       neg=np.argsort(g)[:P]
       impr=0
       # local function loop? We'll write repeated code via a helper `_process_candidate` top-level? It would need many variables and return updated values; cumbersome. Could define a top-level helper that takes arrays and scalars and returns (impr, cmax, active_idx, maybe h/c updated). But Python passing many okay. To avoid duplicates, define `_candidate_sweep(h,c,workA,workV,g,pos,neg,i_random, ... )`? Maybe too many.
```
We can implement a helper `_process_pairs` that receives a list of pairs and all state, returns cmax, active_idx, impr. It can update h,c in place. This keeps code concise.
```
def _process_pairs(h,c,workA,workV,pairs,g,cmax,active_idx,act_tol,iters,active_tol):
    impr=0
    for (i,j) in pairs:
       if i==j: continue
       old_i=h[i]; old_j=h[j]
       lo = max(-old_i, old_j - 1.0)
       hi = min(1.0 - old_i, old_j)
       if hi - lo <= 1e-14: continue
       D = _make_A(h,i,j,workA)
       # stationarity
       if len(active_idx) == 0: active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
       if len(active_idx):
          amin = float(workA[active_idx].min()); amax = float(workA[active_idx].max())
          if amin <= 1e-8 and amax >= -1e-8:
             continue
          if amax < -1e-8:
             lo = 0.0
          elif amin > 1e-8:
             hi = 0.0
       tpos = len(h)-1 + D
       tneg = len(h)-1 - D
       delta,val = _line_search_with_A(c, workA, tpos, tneg, workV, lo, hi, iters)
       if val < cmax - 1e-11:
           # update bounds safe
           hi_new=old_i+delta; hj_new=old_j-delta
           if hi_new < 0 or hi_new >1 or hj_new<0 or hj_new>1:
               # recompute delta from clamped? If out of [lo,hi], skip or clamp
               if hi_new < 0: delta = -old_i
               elif hi_new > 1: delta = 1-old_i
               if hj_new < 0: delta = old_j
               elif hj_new > 1: delta = old_j - 1
               hi_new=old_i+delta; hj_new=old_j-delta
               if not (0<=hi_new<=1 and 0<=hj_new<=1): continue
           h[i]=hi_new; h[j]=hj_new
           np.multiply(workA, delta, out=workV)
           np.add(c, workV, out=c)
           q=delta*delta; c[tpos]+=q; c[tneg]+=q
           cmax = float(c.max())
           active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
           impr += 1
           # recompute c maybe if abs(sum-target)>? no.
    return cmax, active_idx, impr
```
But `_process_pairs` takes pairs list; constructing list each sweep. Good.

Let's implement `_fix_sum` to enforce exactly without large changes:
```
def _fix_sum(h):
   n=len(h); target=0.5*n; s=h.sum(); diff=target - s
   if abs(diff) <= 1e-12: return h
   # adjust entries with room
   if diff > 0:
      order=np.argsort(1.0 - h)  # most room? Actually descending room
   else: order=np.argsort(h)
   # Try up to n entries small
   rem=diff
   for idx in order:
      if rem <=0 or rem>=0? ...
      room = 1.0-h[idx] if rem>0 else h[idx]
      add = min(room, rem) if rem>0 else -min(room, -rem)
      h[idx]+=add; rem-=add
      if abs(rem)<1e-15: break
   if abs(rem)>1e-12:
      return _repair(h,n)
   return h
```
If all at bounds and diff not zero? Shouldn't. This may change objective very tiny.

`_repair` from previous can be used for random. It scales and distributes. Good.

`_downsample_factor(h, f)`: if len % f !=0, f = largest divisor? We can just use if n%f==0 else nearest? For parent 560 factors. For random not. Implement:
```
def _downsample(h,f):
   n=len(h); m=n//f
   if m*f != n:
       h = h[:m*f]
   return h.reshape(m,f).mean(axis=1)
```
`_upsample(h,f)`: repeat f.

def _random_start(n, rng, kind=0):
    if n <=2: return np.full(n,0.5)
    h=np.zeros(n)
    if kind==0: # smoothed binary
       idx=rng.choice(n, n//2, replace=False)
       h[idx]=1.0
       # smooth circular with 9 kernel maybe
       for k in range(1,4):
          h = h + np.roll(h,k) + np.roll(h,-k)
       h /= (2*3+1) maybe? Actually start binary sum n/2; after smoothing sum preserved (rolling sum of h each point =? Each roll sum same, so h_new sum =7*sum /7 preserved. Good.
       h /= 7.0
    elif kind==1:
       h=0.5*np.ones(n)
       x=np.arange(n)*2*np.pi/n
       for r in range(4):
          freq=rng.randint(2, max(3, n//10)+1)
          ph=rng.rand()*2*np.pi
          amp=rng.rand()*0.25
          h += amp*np.sin(x*freq+ph)
    else:
       # block random
       nb = rng.randint(5, 21)
       block = np.block? no. h= (rng.rand(nb)>0.5).astype(float); h=np.repeat(h, n//nb +?) then trim.
       # simpler: h = (np.arange(n) % rng.randint(5,17) < rng.randint(1,3))
       mod=rng.randint(3,17)
       on=rng.randint(1, mod)
       h = (np.arange(n) % mod < on).astype(float)
    return _repair(h,n)
```

Time allocation in run specifics. Need ensure no variable `initial_h_values` if not defined. Use try.
```
def run(seed=42,budget_s=1100,**kwargs):
   t0=time.time(); B=float(budget_s); end=t0+B-1.0
   rng=np.random.RandomState(seed)
   parent=None
   try:
      parent=np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
   except Exception:
      parent=None
   if parent is not None and len(parent)>0:
      npar=len(parent); parent=_repair(parent,npar)
   else:
      npar=560; parent=_random_start(npar,rng,0)
   best_h=parent.copy(); best_c5=_eval(parent)
   # if parent c5 high? maybe quick random 560? no.
   # tasks as list of (h, stop)
   tasks=[]
   # parent
   tasks.append((parent.copy(), t0+0.30*B))
   # downsample

```
def _process_pairs(h, c, workA, workV, pairs, cmax, active_idx, stop, line_iters, act_tol=1e-10, station_tol=1e-8):
    n=len(h); impr=0
    for idx_par,(i,j) in enumerate(pairs):
       if time.time() > stop: break
       if i==j: continue
       old_i=h[i]; old_j=h[j]
       lo = ...
       if hi - lo <= 1e-14: continue
       D = _make_A(h,i,j,workA)
       tpos = n-1+D; tneg=n-1-D
       if len(active_idx)==0:
           active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
       if len(active_idx):
           amin = float(workA[active_idx].min())
           amax = float(workA[active_idx].max())
           if amin <= station_tol and amax >= -station_tol:
               continue
           if amax < -station_tol:
               lo = 0.0
           elif amin > station_tol:
               hi = 0.0
       # If after restriction hi<=lo? if interval contains 0 only and derivative? adjust
       if hi - lo <= 1e-13: continue
       delta, val = _line_search_with_A(c, workA, tpos, tneg, workV, lo, hi, line_iters)
       if val < cmax - 1e-10:
           hi_new=old_i+delta; hj_new=old_j-delta
           if hi_new < 0.0 - 1e-12 or hi_new > 1.0 + 1e-12 or hj_new < -...:
               continue
           if hi_new <0: hi_new=0; delta=-old_i
           elif hi_new>1: hi_new=1; delta=1-old_i
           if hj_new<0: hj_new=0; delta=old_j
           elif hj_new>1: hj_new=1; delta=old_j-1
           if hi_new <0 or hi_new>1 or hj_new<0 or hj_new>1: continue
           # if delta changed significantly, val may not exact. If abs(old_i + delta - (old_i+delta))? Actually delta recomputed from clamped; if change >1e-9, we could recompute c via full? Simpler if clamped, accept but recompute c? We can just continue because line search should give within bounds; clamping only for round.
           h[i]=hi_new; h[j]=hj_new
           np.multiply(workA, delta, out=workV)
           np.add(c, workV, out=c)
           q=delta*delta
           c[tpos] += q
           c[tneg] += q
           cmax = float(c.max())
           active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
           impr +=1
    return cmax, active_idx, impr
```

Can simplify `_repair`: Use projection via `proj_to_sum` after scaling? For initial repair:
```
def _repair(h,n):
    h=np.asarray(h,float).copy(); h=np.clip(h,0,1)
    target=n*0.5; s=h.sum()
    if s<=0: return np.full(n,0.5)
    if abs(s-target)>1e-9:
       h *= target/s; h=np.clip(h,0,1)
       h = _proj_to_sum(h,target) # but proj uses binary search shift, maybe exact.
    return h
```
But `_proj_to_sum` with shift may not preserve some values? It returns exact sum. We can use it. Need target if sum already okay? Use projection if diff >1e-12.
```
def _proj_to_sum(h,target):
   lo,hi=-2.0,2.0
   for _ in range(60):
      mid=(lo+hi)*0.5
      if np.clip(h-mid,0,1).sum() > target: lo=mid
      else: hi=mid
   return np.clip(h-(lo+hi)*0.5,0,1)
```
This returns sum target approximately. Good.

def _fix_sum(h):
   n=len(h); target=0.5*n; diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)<=1e-12: return h
   if abs(diff) <= 0.5: # distribute
      order = np.argsort(1-h) if diff>0 else np.argsort(h)
      for idx in order: ...
      if abs(h.sum()-target)<=1e-10: return h
   return _proj_to_sum(h,target)
```

```
def _fix_sum(h):
   n=len(h); target=0.5*n; diff=target-h.sum()
   if abs(diff)<1e-12: return h
   idx = int(np.argmax(1.0-h)) if diff>0 else int(np.argmax(h))
   room = 1.0-h[idx] if diff>0 else h[idx]
   if abs(diff) <= room + 1e-12:
      h[idx]+=diff; np.clip(h,0,1,out=h); return h
   return _proj_to_sum(h,target)
```

Detailed helper code:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _repair(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).copy()
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    target = 0.5 * n
    s = h.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12:
        h[:] = 0.5
        return h
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-9:
        return _fix_sum(h)
    h *= target / s
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return _proj_to_sum(h, target)
```
But `_fix_sum` may call `_proj_to_sum`, which is defined later? In Python, functions can call later defined as long as called after definition. At runtime all defined. Good.

`_proj_to_sum`:
```
def _proj_to_sum(h, target):
    lo, hi = -2.0, 2.0
    for _ in range(60):
        mid = 0.5*(lo+hi)
        s = np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return np.clip(h - 0.5*(lo+hi), 0.0, 1.0)
```
This returns new array. If target half-integer, binary search exact? It converges to threshold; values at threshold multiple can make sum slightly off, but 60 bits enough. To ensure exact sum? We can call `_fix_sum` after? But projection used in repair; if sum off 1e-12 okay. For half-integer and flat, projection yields some combination arbitrary from shift, may produce sum within 1e-12. Good.

`_fix_sum`:
```
def _fix_sum(h):
    n = len(h)
    target = 0.5 * n
    diff = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-12:
        return h
    if diff > 0:
        idx = int(np.argmax(1.0 - h))
    else:
        idx = int(np.argmax(h))
    room = 1.0 - h[idx] if diff > 0 else h[idx]
    if abs(diff) <= room + 1e-12:
        h[idx] += diff
        if h[idx] < 0.0: h[idx]=0.0
        elif h[idx] > 1.0: h[idx]=1.0
        return h
    return _proj_to_sum(h, target)
```
If diff large but all full/empty due infeasible? Shouldn't.

`_full_c` and `_eval` as above. For eval, `c = np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')`; if n len. Return float(c.max() * 2.0 / n).

`_downsample_factor`:
```
def _down_factor(h,f):
    n=len(h); m=n//f
    if m <=0: return h.copy()
    if m*f < n: h=h[:m*f]
    return h.reshape(m,f).mean(axis=1).copy()
```

`_upsample`:
```
def _upsample(h,f):
    return np.repeat(h, f).copy()
```

`_random_start`:
```
def _random_start(n, rng, kind=0):
    if n < 4: return np.full(n, 0.5)
    if kind == 0:
       h = np.zeros(n)
       half = int(round(n*0.5)) # but if n odd round? target n/2 maybe .5. Use n//2? For n odd, half=n//2+? Sum target. Let's use half = int(n//2). Then repair adjusts.
       h[rng.choice(n, half, replace=False)] = 1.0
       k = max(1, min(3, n//4))
       acc = h.copy()
       for s in range(1, k+1):
           acc += np.roll(h, s) + np.roll(h, -s)
       h = acc / (2*k + 1)
    elif kind == 1:
       h = 0.5 * np.ones(n)
       x = np.arange(n) * (2.0*np.pi / n)
       for _ in range(4):
           f = int(rng.randint(2, max(3, n//12)))
           ph = float(rng.rand()) * 2.0 * np.pi
           amp = float(rng.rand()) * 0.35
           h += amp * np.sin(x * f + ph)
    else:
       mod = int(rng.randint(3, max(4, n//8)))
       on = int(rng.randint(1, mod))
       h = (np.arange(n) % mod < on).astype(float)
    return _repair(h, n)
```
If `n//12` for n small, max. `rng.randint(2, max(3,n//12))` if high=low? If n//12=1, high=max(3,1)=3, range [2,3); gives 2. Good. For binary half if n odd, half=n//2, projection.

`_grad_active` careful with large K:
```
def _grad_active(h,c,k):
    n=len(h); g=np.zeros(n); L=len(c)
    if k <=0: return g
    if k >= L: idx = np.arange(L)
    else: idx = np.argsort(c)[-k:]
    for t in idx:
       d = t - (n-1)
       if d >= 0:
          m = n - d
          if m <=0: continue
          g[d:n] += 1.0 - h[:m]
          g[:m] -= h[d:n]
       else:
          m = n + d  # since d negative, n - (-d)? if d=-e, m=n-e = n+d
          if m <=0: continue
          e = -d
          g[:m] += 1.0 - h[e:n]
          g[e:n] -= h[:m]
    return g
```
Check for d=0: m=n; g[0:n]+=1-h[:n]; g[:n]-=h[0:n] => 1-2h. Good.
For d<0: e=-d, m=n-e = n+d (negative d). g[:m]+=1-h[e:n] length m; g[e:n]-=h[:m] length m. Good.

`_make_A`:
```
def _make_A(h,i,j,workA):
    n=len(h)
    workA.fill(0.0)
    omr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
    workA[i:i+n] += omr
    workA[n-1-i:n-1+i+1] -= h
    workA[j:j+n] -= omr
    workA[n-1-j:n-1+j+1] += h
    return abs(i-j)
```
Potential view with negative stride in omr and adding to overlapping slices? `omr` separate array. Good.

`_line_value`:
```
def _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,delta):
    np.multiply(A, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    m = float(work.max())
    q = delta*delta
    if q > 0.0:
        v = work[t1] + q
        if v > m: m = v
        if t2 != t1:
           v = work[t2] + q
           if v > m: m = v
    return m
```
If delta negative, q positive. Good.

`_line_search_with_A`:
```
def _line_search_with_A(c,A,t1,t2,work,lo,hi,it):
    if hi - lo <= 1e-13:
       d = 0.5*(lo+hi)
       return d, _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,d)
    gr = 0.5*(3.0 - np.sqrt(5.0))
    c1 = hi - gr*(hi-lo)
    c2 = lo + gr*(hi-lo)
    f1 = _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,c1)
    f2 = _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,c2)
    for _ in range(it):
       if f1 < f2:
          hi = c2; c2=c1; f2=f1
          c1 = hi - gr*(hi-lo)
          f1 = _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,c1)
       else:
          lo = c1; c1=c2; f1=f2
          c2 = lo + gr*(hi-lo)
          f2 = _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,c2)
    dm = 0.5*(lo+hi)
    fm = _line_value(work,c,A,t1,t2,dm)
    if f1 < f2:
       if fm < f1: return dm, fm
       return c1, f1
    if fm < f2: return dm, fm
    return c2, f2
```
If hi/lo restricted to [0,hi], f(0)=cmax, but we don't explicitly evaluate 0; golden endpoints not include 0 unless lo=0? Golden interval endpoints lo=0 but f at lo never evaluated unless it gets endpoint? Golden maintains interior points; if minimum at boundary lo, after iterations interval shrinks to boundary but function value maybe from interior near boundary; could be slightly higher than boundary? Convex min at boundary, golden will approach boundary, c1/c2 near lo, f near f(lo). Good. But if f(lo)=cmax and derivative negative (restricted because amax<0 => min positive not at 0), fine. If restricted to [lo,0] min at 0, near 0. Good.
Could check endpoints explicitly to be safe:
```
flo = _line_value(...,lo); fhi=...
# But would overwrite work; okay. Add 2 evals. Could help if min boundary.
```
Let's add endpoint evaluation and include in candidates? It adds 2 calls per line, minor. But work overwritten; we can evaluate after golden. Need not store all? We can evaluate at end and compare.
At end, evaluate lo,hi, c1,c2,dm maybe 5 evals; but c1/c2 already values? Work overwritten. We can compute `f_lo`, `f_hi`, and compare with f1/f2. But f1/f2 values from previous evaluations are valid; no need recompute. To ensure boundary min, add:
```
flo = _line_value(...,lo); fhi = _line_value(...,hi)
if flo < fbest: return lo, flo
if fhi < fbest: return hi, fhi
```

`_perturb_around`:
```
def _perturb_around(h, amp, rng, target):
    n=len(h)
    hp=h.copy()
    m=max(1, min(n, int(0.15*n)))
    idx = rng.choice(n, m, replace=False)
    hp[idx] += amp * rng.randn(m)
    return _proj_to_sum(hp, target)
```
This creates new array. If amp grows, projection.

`_process_pairs` as above. Need `time` import at top.

`_coord_opt` detailed:
```
def _coord_opt(h, t0, stop, rng, line_iters=None, max_restarts=12):
    n = len(h)
    h = _repair(h, n)
    c = _full_c(h)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cmax
    target = 0.5*n
    L = 2*n-1
    workA = np.zeros(L); workV = np.zeros(L)
    if line_iters is None:
       line_iters = 22 if n < 300 else 24
    if n < 100:
       P=3; Rpairs=40; topK=8; base_pamp=0.18
    elif n < 250:
       P=5; Rpairs=70; topK=12; base_pamp=0.12
    else:
       P=7; Rpairs=110; topK=18; base_pamp=0.07
    act_tol = 1e-7
    active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
    no_impr=0; restart=0
    while time.time() < stop:
       g = _grad_active(h,c, topK)
       pos = np.argsort(g)[-P:]
       neg = np.argsort(g)[:P]
       pairs=[]; seen=set()
       # add pos-neg
       for a in pos:
          for b in neg:
             if a != b:
                aa = a if a < b else b; bb = b if a < b else a
                key = aa*n + bb
                if key not in seen:
                   seen.add(key); pairs.append((aa,bb))
       # extreme h
       hi1 = int(np.argmax(h)); lo1=int(np.argmin(h)); add if needed
       # random shifted
       rmax = max(2, n//3)
       for _ in range(Rpairs):
          a = int(rng.randint(n))
          r = int(rng.randint(1, rmax+1))
          b = (a + r) % n
          if a != b:
             aa = a if a < b else b; bb = b if a < b else a
             key = aa*n + bb
             if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key); pairs.append((aa,bb))
       cmax, active_idx, impr = _process_pairs(h,c,workA,workV,pairs,cmax,active_idx,stop,line_iters,act_tol)
       if impr > 0:
           no_impr = 0
           if cmax < best_c:
              best_c = cmax; best_h = h.copy()
       else:
           no_impr += 1
           if no_impr >= 2:
               amp = base_pamp * (1.0 + 0.05*restart)
               h = _perturb_around(best_h, amp, rng, target)
               c = _full_c(h)
               cmax = float(c.max())
               active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
               if cmax < best_c:
                  best_c = cmax; best_h = h.copy()
               no_impr = 0; restart += 1
               if restart > max_restarts:
                  break
    if cmax < best_c:
       best_c = cmax; best_h = h.copy()
    best_h = _fix_sum(best_h)
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c5
```
Need add extreme pair code without nested add. We'll write repeated:
```
if hi1 != lo1:
   aa=hi1 if hi1<lo1 else lo1; bb=lo1 if hi1<lo1 else hi1; key=aa*n+bb
   if key not in seen: ...
```
Could add `min_h` and max_g? `pos` already includes gradient extremes; okay.

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    B = float(budget_s)
    end = t0 + B - 1.5
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    if parent is None or len(parent) < 8:
        npar = 560
        parent = _random_start(npar, rng, 0)
    else:
        npar = len(parent)
        parent = _repair(parent, npar)
    best_h = parent.copy()
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)
    # If B very small, just parent quick
    if B < 60:
       hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(parent, t0, end, rng, line_iters=14, max_restarts=2)
       if c5 < best_c5: best...
       return _fix_sum(...)
    tasks = []
    # fractions depend on npar maybe
    if npar >= 100:
       # parent high (if npar <= 700, else maybe downsample high)
       tasks.append((parent.copy(), t0 + 0.26*B))
       if npar % 2 ==0 or npar//2 >= 20: tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 2), t0 + 0.40*B))
       if npar % 4 ==0 or npar//4 >= 20: tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 4), t0 + 0.52*B))
       if npar % 8 ==0 or npar//8 >= 20: tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 8), t0 + 0.62*B))
       # random low
       n2 = max(24, npar//4)
       tasks.append((_random_start(n2, rng, 0), t0 + 0.72*B))
       n3 = max(20, npar//8)
       tasks.append((_random_start(n3, rng, 1), t0 + 0.80*B))
       # if npar not huge random high medium
       nmed = max(24, npar//2)
       tasks.append((_random_start(nmed, rng, 0), t0 + 0.88*B))
    else:
       tasks.append((parent.copy(), t0 + 0.5*B))
    for h0, stop in tasks:
       if time.time() >= end - 20: break
       if stop > end - 2: stop = end - 2
       hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(h0, t0, stop, rng)
       if c5 < best_c5:
          best_c5 = c5; best_h = hopt
    # Upsample best if low and time
    if len(best_h) < npar and npar % len(best_h) == 0 and end - time.time() > 150:
       f = npar // len(best_h)
       h_up = _upsample(best_h, f)
       h_up = _repair(h_up, npar)
       stop_up = end - 60
       hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(h_up, t0, stop_up, rng, line_iters=24, max_restarts=40)
       if c5 < best_c5:
          best_c5 = c5; best_h = hopt
    # final polish
    if time.time() < end - 3:
       iters = 26 if len(best_h) >= 250 else 22
       hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(best_h, t0, end - 1.0, rng, line_iters=iters, max_restarts=80)
       if c5 < best_c5:
          best_c5 = c5; best_h = hopt
    best_h = _fix_sum(best_h)
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c5, len(best_h)
```

Let's refine `_process_pairs` for speed and correctness:
```
def _process_pairs(h, c, workA, workV, pairs, cmax, active_idx, stop, iters, tol=1e-8):
    n = len(h)
    impr = 0
    for p in pairs:
        if time.time() > stop: break
        i = p[0]; j = p[1]
        if i == j: continue
        oi = h[i]; oj = h[j]
        lo = -oi
        t = oj - 1.0
        if t > lo: lo = t
        hi = 1.0 - oi
        if oj < hi: hi = oj
        if hi - lo <= 1e-14: continue
        d = _make_A(h, i, j, workA)
        t1 = n - 1 + d
        t2 = n - 1 - d
        if len(active_idx) == 0:
            active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - tol)
        if len(active_idx):
            aact = workA[active_idx]
            amin = float(aact.min()); amax = float(aact.max())
            if amin <= tol and amax >= -tol:
                continue
            if amax < -tol:
                lo = 0.0
            elif amin > tol:
                hi = 0.0
        if hi - lo <= 1e-13:
            continue
        delta, val = _line_search_with_A(c, workA, t1, t2, workV, lo, hi, iters)
        if val < cmax - 1e-9:
            ni = oi + delta; nj = oj - delta
            if ni < 0.0: ni = 0.0; delta = -oi
            elif ni > 1.0: ni = 1.0; delta = 1.0 - oi
            if nj < 0.0: nj = 0.0; delta = oj
            elif nj > 1.0: nj = 1.0; delta = oj - 1.0
            if ni < 0.0 or ni > 1.0 or nj < 0.0 or nj > 1.0:
                continue
            h[i] = ni; h[j] = nj
            np.multiply(workA, delta, out=workV)
            np.add(c, workV, out=c)
            q = delta * delta
            c[t1] += q
            c[t2] += q
            cmax = float(c.max())
            active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - tol)
            impr += 1
    return cmax, active_idx, impr
```

Let's strengthen `_fix_sum`:
```
def _fix_sum(h):
    n=len(h); target=0.5*n; diff=target-h.sum()
    if abs(diff)<=1e-14: return h
    if abs(diff) <= 0.25:
       order = np.argsort(1-h) if diff>0 else np.argsort(h)
       rem=diff
       for idx in order:
          if abs(rem)<=1e-14: break
          if rem>0:
             add=min(rem,1-h[idx])
          else: add=max(rem,h[idx])
          h[idx]+=add; rem-=add
       if abs(rem)<=1e-10: return h
    return _proj_to_sum(h,target)
```
But if diff large >0.25? sum infeasible? random projection. Use no threshold; loop over all entries distributes. It can be O(n) per final, okay. For small diff, one entry. Let's implement simple distribute for all if one-entry not enough:
```
if abs(diff) <= 0.5:
   ... loop
   if abs(h.sum()-target)<=1e-10: return h
return _proj_to_sum
```
This changes more entries but tiny. Good.

Let's examine `_make_A` with `omr` and slices:
```
omr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
workA[i:i+n] += omr

```
If i/j are numpy ints, slice endpoints okay. If i+n > L? max i=n-1 -> 2n-1, L=2n-1, end exclusive ok. `n-1+j+1` = n+j <= 2n-1. Good.

Let's test with n=3 h [a,b,c], i=1,j=2. E1: first t=1..3 omr [c,b,a] => t1 c,t2 b,t3 a. second start=1 end=4 (indices1,2,3) -= h [a,b,c] => t1 -a,t2 -b,t3 -c. def _fix_sum(h):
   n=len(h); target=0.5*n; diff=target-float(h.sum())
   if abs(diff) <=1e-13: return h
   if diff > 0: order=np.argsort(1.0-h)
   else: order=np.argsort(h)
   rem=diff
   for idx in order:
       if abs(rem) <= 1e-13: break
       if rem > 0:
           add = min(rem, 1.0-h[idx])
       else:
           add = max(rem, h[idx])
       h[idx] += add
       rem -= add
   if abs(rem) <= 1e-10:
       np.clip(h,0.0,1.0,out=h)
       return h
   return _proj_to_sum(h,target)
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

_GOLD = 0.5 * (3.0 - np.sqrt(5.0))
_TAU = 2.0 * np.pi

def _proj_to_sum(h, target):
    lo, hi = -2.0, 2.0
    for _ in range(60):
        mid = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        s = np.clip(h - mid, 0.0, 1.0).sum()
        if s > target:
            lo = mid
        else:
            hi = mid
    return np.clip(h - 0.5 * (lo + hi), 0.0, 1.0)

def _fix_sum(h):
    n = len(h)
    target = 0.5 * n
    diff = target - float(h.sum())
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-13:
        return h
    if diff > 0.0:
        order = np.argsort(1.0 - h)
    else:
        order = np.argsort(h)
    rem = diff
    for idx in order:
        if abs(rem) <= 1e-13:
            break
        if rem > 0.0:
            add = min(rem, 1.0 - h[idx])
        else:
            add = max(rem, h[idx])
        h[idx] += add
        rem -= add
    if abs(rem) <= 1e-10:
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
        return h
    return _proj_to_sum(h, target)

def _repair(h, n):
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=float).ravel().copy()
    if h.size != n:
        h = h[:n]
        if h.size < n:
            h = np.concatenate((h, np.zeros(n - h.size)))
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    target = 0.5 * n
    s = float(h.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        h[:] = 0.5
        return h
    diff = target - s
    if abs(diff) <= 1e-9:
        return _fix_sum(h)
    h *= target / s
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return _fix_sum(_proj_to_sum(h, target))

def _full_c(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full")

def _eval(h):
    n = len(h)
    return float(np.max(_full_c(h)) * 2.0 / n)

def _down_factor(h, f):
    n = len(h)
    m = max(1, n // f)
    if m * f < n:
        h = h[:m * f]
    return h.reshape(m, f).mean(axis=1).copy()

def _upsample(h, f):
    return np.repeat(h, f).copy()

def _random_start(n, rng, kind=0):
    if n < 4:
        return np.full(n, 0.5)
    if kind == 0:
        h = np.zeros(n)
        half = n // 2
        if half > 0:
            h[rng.choice(n, half, replace=False)] = 1.0
        k = max(1, min(3, n // 4))
        acc = h.copy()
        for s in range(1, k + 1):
            acc += np.roll(h, s) + np.roll(h, -s)
        h = acc / (2 * k + 1)
    elif kind == 1:
        h = 0.5 * np.ones(n)
        x = np.arange(n) * (_TAU / n)
        for _ in range(4):
            f = int(rng.randint(2, max(3, n // 12)))
            ph = float(rng.rand()) * _TAU
            amp = float(rng.rand()) * 0.35
            h += amp * np.sin(x * f + ph)
    else:
        mod = int(rng.randint(3, max(4, n // 8)))
        on = int(rng.randint(1, mod))
        h = (np.arange(n) % mod < on).astype(float)
    return _repair(h, n)

def _grad_active(h, c, k):
    n = len(h)
    g = np.zeros(n)
    L = len(c)
    if k <= 0:
        return g
    if k >= L:
        idx = np.arange(L)
    else:
        idx = np.argsort(c)[-k:]
    for t in idx:
        d = t - (n - 1)
        if d >= 0:
            m = n - d
            if m <= 0:
                continue
            g[d:n] += 1.0 - h[:m]
            g[:m] -= h[d:n]
        else:
            e = -d
            m = n - e
            if m <= 0:
                continue
            g[:m] += 1.0 - h[e:n]
            g[e:n] -= h[:m]
    return g

def _make_A(h, i, j, workA):
    n = len(h)
    workA.fill(0.0)
    omr = 1.0 - h[::-1]
    workA[i:i + n] += omr
    workA[n - 1 - i:n - 1 + i + 1] -= h
    workA[j:j + n] -= omr
    workA[n - 1 - j:n - 1 + j + 1] += h
    return abs(i - j)

def _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, delta):
    np.multiply(A, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    m = float(work.max())
    q = delta * delta
    if q > 0.0:
        v = work[t1] + q
        if v > m:
            m = v
        if t2 != t1:
            v = work[t2] + q
            if v > m:
                m = v
    return m

def _line_search_with_A(c, A, t1, t2, work, lo, hi, it):
    span = hi - lo
    if span <= 1e-13:
        d = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
        return d, _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, d)
    if span > 1e-10:
        lo += 1e-12 * span
        hi -= 1e-12 * span
        if hi - lo <= 1e-13:
            d = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
            return d, _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, d)
    c1 = hi - _GOLD * (hi - lo)
    c2 = lo + _GOLD * (hi - lo)
    f1 = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, c1)
    f2 = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, c2)
    for _ in range(it):
        if f1 < f2:
            hi = c2
            c2 = c1
            f2 = f1
            c1 = hi - _GOLD * (hi - lo)
            f1 = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, c1)
        else:
            lo = c1
            c1 = c2
            f1 = f2
            c2 = lo + _GOLD * (hi - lo)
            f2 = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, c2)
    dm = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
    best_d, best_f = dm, _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, dm)
    if f1 < best_f:
        best_d, best_f = c1, f1
    if f2 < best_f:
        best_d, best_f = c2, f2
    fl = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, lo)
    if fl < best_f:
        best_d, best_f = lo, fl
    fh = _line_value(work, c, A, t1, t2, hi)
    if fh < best_f:
        best_d, best_f = hi, fh
    return best_d, best_f

def _perturb_around(h, amp, rng, target):
    n = len(h)
    hp = h.copy()
    m = max(1, min(n, int(0.18 * n)))
    idx = rng.choice(n, m, replace=False)
    hp[idx] += amp * rng.randn(m)
    return _fix_sum(_proj_to_sum(hp, target))

def _process_pairs(h, c, workA, workV, pairs, cmax, active_idx, stop, iters, tol=1e-8):
    n = len(h)
    impr = 0
    for p in pairs:
        if time.time() > stop:
            break
        i = p[0]
        j = p[1]
        if i == j:
            continue
        oi = h[i]
        oj = h[j]
        lo = max(-oi, oj - 1.0)
        hi = min(1.0 - oi, oj)
        if hi - lo <= 1e-14:
            continue
        d = _make_A(h, i, j, workA)
        t1 = n - 1 + d
        t2 = n - 1 - d
        if len(active_idx) == 0:
            active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - tol)
        if len(active_idx):
            aa = workA[active_idx]
            amin = float(aa.min())
            amax = float(aa.max())
            if amin <= tol and amax >= -tol:
                continue
            if amax < -tol:
                lo = 0.0
            elif amin > tol:
                hi = 0.0
        if hi - lo <= 1e-13:
            continue
        delta, val = _line_search_with_A(c, workA, t1, t2, workV, lo, hi, iters)
        if val < cmax - 1e-9:
            ni = oi + delta
            nj = oj - delta
            if ni < 0.0:
                ni = 0.0
                delta = -oi
            elif ni > 1.0:
                ni = 1.0
                delta = 1.0 - oi
            if nj < 0.0:
                nj = 0.0
                delta = oj
            elif nj > 1.0:
                nj = 1.0
                delta = oj - 1.0
            if ni < 0.0 or ni > 1.0 or nj < 0.0 or nj > 1.0:
                continue
            h[i] = ni
            h[j] = nj
            np.multiply(workA, delta, out=workV)
            np.add(c, workV, out=c)
            q = delta * delta
            c[t1] += q
            c[t2] += q
            cmax = float(c.max())
            active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - tol)
            impr += 1
    return cmax, active_idx, impr

def _coord_opt(h, stop, rng, line_iters=None, max_restarts=12, P=None, Rpairs=None):
    n = len(h)
    h = _repair(h, n)
    c = _full_c(h)
    cmax = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = cmax
    target = 0.5 * n
    L = 2 * n - 1
    workA = np.zeros(L)
    workV = np.zeros(L)
    if line_iters is None:
        line_iters = 22 if n < 300 else 24
    if n < 100:
        P0, R0, K0, amp0 = 3, 45, 8, 0.20
    elif n < 250:
        P0, R0, K0, amp0 = 5, 80, 12, 0.13
    else:
        P0, R0, K0, amp0 = 7, 120, 18, 0.07
    if P is None:
        P = P0
    if Rpairs is None:
        Rpairs = R0
    act_tol = 1e-8
    active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
    no_impr = 0
    restart = 0
    while time.time() < stop:
        g = _grad_active(h, c, K0)
        pos = np.argsort(g)[-P:]
        neg = np.argsort(g)[:P]
        pairs = []
        seen = set()
        for a0 in pos:
            for b0 in neg:
                if a0 != b0:
                    aa = a0 if a0 < b0 else b0
                    bb = b0 if a0 < b0 else a0
                    key = aa * n + bb
                    if key not in seen:
                        seen.add(key)
                        pairs.append((aa, bb))
        hi_idx = int(np.argmax(h))
        lo_idx = int(np.argmin(h))
        if hi_idx != lo_idx:
            aa = hi_idx if hi_idx < lo_idx else lo_idx
            bb = lo_idx if hi_idx < lo_idx else hi_idx
            key = aa * n + bb
            if key not in seen:
                seen.add(key)
                pairs.append((aa, bb))
        rmax = max(2, n // 3)
        for r in range(Rpairs):
            a0 = int(rng.randint(n))
            if r < (Rpairs // 2):
                b0 = int(rng.randint(n))
                if b0 == a0:
                    b0 = (b0 + 1) % n
            else:
                b0 = (a0 + int(rng.randint(1, rmax + 1))) % n
            if a0 != b0:
                aa = a0 if a0 < b0 else b0
                bb = b0 if a0 < b0 else a0
                key = aa * n + bb
                if key not in seen:
                    seen.add(key)
                    pairs.append((aa, bb))
        cmax, active_idx, impr = _process_pairs(h, c, workA, workV, pairs, cmax, active_idx, stop, line_iters, act_tol)
        if impr > 0:
            no_impr = 0
            if cmax < best_c:
                best_c = cmax
                best_h = h.copy()
        else:
            no_impr += 1
            if no_impr >= 2 and time.time() < stop - 2:
                amp = amp0 * (1.0 + 0.10 * restart)
                if amp > 0.25:
                    amp = 0.25
                h = _perturb_around(best_h, amp, rng, target)
                c = _full_c(h)
                cmax = float(c.max())
                active_idx = np.flatnonzero(c >= cmax - act_tol)
                if cmax < best_c:
                    best_c = cmax
                    best_h = h.copy()
                no_impr = 0
                restart += 1
                if restart > max_restarts:
                    break
    if cmax < best_c:
        best_c = cmax
        best_h = h.copy()
    best_h = _fix_sum(best_h)
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c5

def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    B = float(budget_s)
    end = t0 + B - 1.5
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed)
    parent = None
    try:
        iv = initial_h_values
        if iv is not None:
            parent = np.asarray(iv, dtype=float).ravel().copy()
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    if parent is None or parent.size < 8:
        npar = 560
        parent = _random_start(npar, rng, 0)
    else:
        npar = int(parent.size)
        parent = _repair(parent, npar)
    best_h = parent.copy()
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)

    tasks = []
    if B > 500:
        tasks.append((parent.copy(), t0 + 0.25 * B))
        if npar // 2 >= 20:
            tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 2), t0 + 0.38 * B))
        if npar // 4 >= 20:
            tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 4), t0 + 0.50 * B))
        if npar // 8 >= 20:
            tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 8), t0 + 0.60 * B))
        nq = max(20, npar // 4)
        tasks.append((_random_start(nq, rng, 0), t0 + 0.70 * B))
        nh = max(20, npar // 8)
        tasks.append((_random_start(nh, rng, 1), t0 + 0.78 * B))
        nm = max(30, npar // 2)
        tasks.append((_random_start(nm, rng, 0), t0 + 0.86 * B))
    elif B > 200:
        tasks.append((parent.copy(), t0 + 0.35 * B))
        if npar // 2 >= 20:
            tasks.append((_down_factor(parent, 2), t0 + 0.55 * B))
        nq = max(20, npar // 4)
        tasks.append((_random_start(nq, rng, 0), t0 + 0.75 * B))
    else:
        tasks.append((parent.copy(), end - 4.0))

    for h0, stop in tasks:
        if time.time() >= end - 20:
            break
        if stop > end - 2.0:
            stop = end - 2.0
        hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(h0, stop, rng)
        if c5 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = c5
            best_h = hopt

    if len(best_h) < npar and end - time.time() > 120:
        f = max(1, npar // len(best_h))
        h_up = _upsample(best_h, f)
        if len(h_up) < npar:
            h_up = np.concatenate((h_up, h_up[:npar - len(h_up)]))
        elif len(h_up) > npar:
            h_up = h_up[:npar]
        h_up = _repair(h_up, npar)
        hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(h_up, end - 50.0, rng, line_iters=24, max_restarts=35, P=8, Rpairs=130)
        if c5 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = c5
            best_h = hopt

    if time.time() < end - 3.0:
        nfin = len(best_h)
        itfin = 26 if nfin >= 250 else 22
        Pfin = 9 if nfin >= 250 else 6
        Rfin = 160 if nfin >= 250 else 90
        hopt, c5 = _coord_opt(best_h, end - 1.0, rng, line_iters=itfin, max_restarts=80, P=Pfin, Rpairs=Rfin)
        if c5 < best_c5:
            best_c5 = c5
            best_h = hopt

    best_h = _fix_sum(best_h)
    best_c5 = _eval(best_h)
    return best_h, best_c5, len(best_h)
```
